Completed means the initial curated cross-reference pass: the source verse and every listed target passage were read in the pinned Saints-Translation English records, and each retained pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean every possible biblical connection has been exhausted, or independent scholarly approval.

Judges 1:1

After Joshua’s death, the sons of Israel inquired of the Lord, saying, “Who shall go up for us against the Canaanites, to lead the battle against them?”

  1. Judges 20:18 Repeated inquiry about the leading tribe

    Both accounts have Israel inquire who should lead a battle, with Judah named in the response; the earlier opponents are Canaanites and the later opponents Benjamin, so the campaigns are kept distinct.

    Read matching passage

    Judges 20:18 They arose and went up to Bethel and inquired of God. The sons of Israel said, “Who shall go up leading us to fight Benjamin?” The Lord said, “Judah shall go up as leader.”

    Pinned source record

Judges 1:2

The Lord said, “Judah shall go up. Behold, I have given the land into his hand.”

  1. Judges 20:18 Judah chosen to lead in distinct campaigns

    The Lord appoints Judah to go up in each account; Judges1 additionally promises the land into Judah's hand, while Judges20 concerns the different conflict against Benjamin.

    Read matching passage

    Judges 20:18 They arose and went up to Bethel and inquired of God. The sons of Israel said, “Who shall go up leading us to fight Benjamin?” The Lord said, “Judah shall go up as leader.”

    Pinned source record

Judges 1:3

And Judah said to Simeon his brother, “Go up with me into my allotted territory, and let us fight against the Canaanites; and I also will go with you into your allotted territory.” And Simeon went with him.

  1. Joshua 19:1-9 Judah and Simeon acting within related allotments

    Judah invites Simeon into reciprocal fighting in their allotted territories; Joshua explicitly places Simeon's inheritance amid Judah's lot and explains its derivation from Judah's larger portion.

    Read matching passage

    Joshua 19:1 The second lot came out for the sons of Simeon, and their inheritance lay amid the lots of the sons of Judah.

    Joshua 19:2 Their lot was Beer-sheba, Samaa, and Koladam,

    Joshua 19:3 Arsola, Bola, and Asom,

    Joshua 19:4 Elthoula, Boula, and Hormah,

    Joshua 19:5 Ziklag, Bethmachereb, and Sarsousin,

    Joshua 19:6 Batharoth and their fields: thirteen cities, with their villages.

    Joshua 19:7 Eremmon, Thalcha, Ether, and Ashan: four cities, with their villages

    Joshua 19:8 around their cities as far as Barek, going toward Bameth on the southwest. This is the inheritance of the tribe of the sons of Simeon according to their divisions.

    Joshua 19:9 The inheritance of the tribe of the sons of Simeon came from Judah’s lot, because the portion of the sons of Judah was larger than theirs. So the sons of Simeon inherited amid their lot.

    Pinned source record

Judges 1:4

Judah went up, and the Lord gave the Canaanites and the Perizzites into his hand. He struck them at Bezek: ten thousand men.

  1. Judges 1:2 Same-chapter promised delivery and the Bezek victory

    The Lord's promise to give the land into Judah's hand is followed by the Lord giving the Canaanites and Perizzites into that hand and Judah's stated defeat of ten thousand at Bezek.

    Read matching passage

    Judges 1:2 The Lord said, “Judah shall go up. Behold, I have given the land into his hand.”

    Pinned source record

Judges 1:5

They found Adoni-bezek at Bezek, fought against him, and struck the Canaanites and the Perizzites.

  1. Judges 1:6-7 Same-chapter Adoni-bezek encounter and its outcome

    The encounter with Adoni-bezek at Bezek leads to his flight, capture, mutilation and own explanation of repayment; the later units identify what became of this particular defeated ruler.

    Read matching passage

    Judges 1:6 Adoni-bezek fled, but they pursued him, seized him, and cut off the extremities of his hands and his feet.

    Judges 1:7 Adoni-bezek said, “Seventy kings, with the extremities of their hands and feet cut off, used to gather what was beneath my table. As I have done, so God has repaid me.” They brought him to Jerusalem, and he died there.

    Pinned source record

Judges 1:6

Adoni-bezek fled, but they pursued him, seized him, and cut off the extremities of his hands and his feet.

  1. Judges 1:7 Same-chapter injury and Adoni-bezek's repayment confession

    The cutting of Adoni-bezek's hand and foot extremities is the precise treatment he says he formerly inflicted on seventy kings; his own speech interprets his corresponding treatment as God's repayment.

    Read matching passage

    Judges 1:7 Adoni-bezek said, “Seventy kings, with the extremities of their hands and feet cut off, used to gather what was beneath my table. As I have done, so God has repaid me.” They brought him to Jerusalem, and he died there.

    Pinned source record

  1. Judges 1:6 Same-chapter repayment speech explains the reported injury

    Adoni-bezek's confession about mutilating kings and being repaid directly explains the preceding report that his own hand and foot extremities were cut; no legal endorsement of either action is inferred.

Judges 1:8

The sons of Judah fought against Jerusalem and captured it. They struck it with the edge of the sword and burned the city with fire.

  1. Joshua 15:63 Jerusalem's capture distinguished from Jebusite removal

    Judges reports Judah capturing and burning Jerusalem; Joshua reports Judah's inability to destroy its Jebusites and their continued residence, retaining the different actions without supplying an unstated chronology.

    Read matching passage

    Joshua 15:63 The Jebusites lived in Jerusalem, and the sons of Judah could not destroy them. The Jebusites lived in Jerusalem until that day.

    Pinned source record

Judges 1:9

After these things the sons of Judah went down to fight against the Canaanites dwelling in the hill country, the south, and the plain.

  1. Judges 1:19 Same-chapter hill-country campaign and later possession report

    Judah's campaign includes the hill country, south and plain; the later notice specifically reports mountain inheritance and failure in the valley, preserving its unusual Rechab explanation without importing iron chariots.

    Read matching passage

    Judges 1:19 The Lord was with Judah, and he inherited the mountain, because he could not inherit the inhabitants of the valley, because Rechab had separated it.

Judges 1:10

Judah went against the Canaanites dwelling in Hebron, and Hebron came out against him. Hebron’s former name was Kariatharboksepher. He struck Sheshai, Ahiman, and Talmai, descendants of Anak.

  1. Judges 1:20 Same-chapter Hebron conflict and Caleb's Anakite removal

    The Hebron account names three Anak descendants struck; the later Caleb grant again describes removing three sons of Anak, while retaining its additional three-cities wording and not resolving outside name variants.

    Read matching passage

    Judges 1:20 He gave Hebron to Caleb, as Moses had said, and Caleb inherited the three cities from there and removed from there the three sons of Anak.

Judges 1:11

From there they went against the inhabitants of Debir. Debir’s former name was City of Letters.

  1. Joshua 15:15 Debir's explicit former-name notice in the parallel campaign

    Both campaign accounts turn against Debir's inhabitants and explicitly call its former name City of Letters; Joshua names Caleb as the advancing actor, while Judges continues its preceding narrative.

    Read matching passage

    Joshua 15:15 Caleb went up from there against the inhabitants of Debir; Debir’s former name was City of Letters.

Judges 1:14

And when she entered, he urged her to ask her father for the field. She murmured on the beast of burden and cried out from it, “You have given me away into a southern land.” And Caleb said to her, “What do you desire?”

  1. Joshua 15:18 Achsah's field request scene with differing initiative wording

    Both describe her arrival, a proposed request to her father for a field, her cry from the riding animal and Caleb's question; Judges says he urged her, while Joshua reports her consulting him and speaking.

    Joshua 15:18 When she entered, she consulted with him, saying, “I will ask my father for a field.” She cried out from the donkey, and Caleb said to her, “What is it?”

Judges 1:15

And Achsah said to him, “Give me a blessing; for you have given me away into a southern land. Give me a redemption of water.” And Caleb gave her, according to her heart, the redemption of the heights and the redemption of the low places.

  1. Joshua 15:19 Achsah's requested blessing and upper-lower grant

Judges 1:16

The descendants of Jobab the Kenite, Moses’ father-in-law, went up from the City of Palms to the sons of Judah, into the wilderness in the south, on the descent to Arad. He went and dwelt with the people.

  1. Judges 4:11 Jobab-related Kenite households and their movements

    Both explicitly associate Kenite households with Jobab, Moses' in-law; one describes movement from the City of Palms toward Judah and Arad, while the other describes separation and a tent near Kedesh, without importing a Hobab identity.

    Judges 4:11 The neighbours of the Kenite had separated from the sons of Jobab, Moses’ in-law. He pitched his tent near the Oak of the Resters, near Kedesh.

Judges 1:18

Judah did not possess Gaza and its territory, Ashkelon and its territory, Ekron and its territory, or Ashdod and its surrounding lands.

  1. Joshua 13:3 Unpossessed coastal districts and the remaining Philistine satrapies

    Judges says Judah did not possess Gaza, Ashkelon, Ekron and Ashdod with their districts; Joshua names these four within the five Philistine satrapies in its remaining-land description, also naming Gath there.

Judges 1:20

Judges 1:21

  1. Joshua 15:63 Jerusalem's continuing Jebusites under different tribal statements

    Both describe Jebusites remaining in Jerusalem to the narrated day; Judges attributes non-removal to Benjamin, while Joshua names Judah's inability to destroy them, without merging the tribes or their actions.

Judges 1:27

Manasseh did not possess Beth-shan, which is the City of the Scythians, or its dependent towns or surrounding lands; nor Ekthanaad and its dependent towns, the inhabitants of Dor and its dependent towns, the inhabitants of Balaam and its dependent towns, the inhabitants of Magedon and its dependent towns, or the inhabitants of Jeblaam and its dependent towns. The Canaanites began to dwell in this land.

  1. Joshua 17:11-12 Manasseh town-possession lists and remaining Canaanites

    Both Manasseh accounts include Dor and its settlements and describe unremoved Canaanites; Beth-shan/Beth-shean and Magedon/Megiddo forms and the different complete town lists are preserved without claiming full list identity.

    Read matching passage

    Joshua 17:11 Within Issachar and Asher, Manasseh shall have Beth-shean and their villages, the inhabitants of Dor and its villages, the inhabitants of Megiddo and its villages, and the third part of Napheta and its villages.

    Joshua 17:12 The sons of Manasseh could not destroy these cities, and the Canaanites began to live in this land.

Judges 1:28

When Israel became strong, it subjected the Canaanites to tribute, but did not completely remove them.

  1. Joshua 17:13 Israel's growing strength and subjection without complete removal

    Both state that Israel grew strong and subjected the Canaanites while not completely removing or destroying them; Judges specifies tribute and Joshua retains its more general subjection wording.

    Read matching passage

    Joshua 17:13 When the sons of Israel grew strong, they made the Canaanites subject to them, but did not utterly destroy them.

Judges 1:29

Ephraim did not remove the Canaanites dwelling in Gezer. The Canaanites dwelt among them in Gezer and became subject to tribute.

  1. Joshua 16:10 Ephraim's unremoved Canaanites at Gezer

    Both explicitly say Ephraim did not remove or destroy Gezer's Canaanites, who remained among them; Judges adds tribute, while Joshua supplies its separate later Pharaoh expansion.

    Read matching passage

    Joshua 16:10 Ephraim did not destroy the Canaanites who lived in Gezer. The Canaanites lived in Ephraim to this day, until Pharaoh king of Egypt went up, took it, and set it on fire. They pierced through the Canaanites, Perizzites, and inhabitants of Gezer, and Pharaoh gave it as a dowry to his daughter.

    The general Israel-wide notice of Canaanite tribute without complete removal has a concrete tribal example in Zebulun's Kedron and Enaala report; no alternate identities for those towns are asserted.

  1. Judges 1:32 Same-chapter Asher's named failures and their stated consequence

    The list of places Asher did not remove is followed by Asher dwelling among the Canaanites because it could not remove them; the latter gives the explicit reason for this particular territorial outcome.

Judges 2:1

The messenger of the Lord went up from Gilgal to the Place of Weeping, to Bethel, and to the house of Israel. He said to them, “The Lord, the Lord brought you up from Egypt and brought you into the land that He swore to your fathers to give you. He said to you, ‘I will never scatter My covenant with you.

  1. Exodus 6:6-8 Egyptian deliverance and the sworn ancestral land

    The messenger recalls the Lord bringing Israel out of Egypt into the fathers' sworn land; Exodus promises that same sequence of rescue and the land pledged to Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, preserving the distinct covenant formulations.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 6:6 Go, say to the sons of Israel: ‘I am the Lord. I will bring you out from the Egyptians’ domination and deliver you from slavery. I will ransom you with an uplifted arm and with great judgment.

    Exodus 6:7 And I will take you to Myself for a people, and I will be your God; and you shall know that I am the Lord your God, who brought you out from the oppression of the Egyptians.

    Exodus 6:8 I will bring you into the land concerning which I stretched out My hand to give it to Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob. I will give it to you as an inheritance. I am the Lord.’”

    Pinned source record

Judges 2:2

You shall make no covenant with those dwelling in this land, nor shall you bow down to their gods; but you shall shatter their graven images and pull down their altars.’ Yet you did not obey My voice when you did these things.

  1. Exodus 23:32-33 Prohibited covenants and service of inhabitants' gods

    Judges recalls the prohibition of covenants with the land's inhabitants and worship of their gods; Exodus explicitly forbids those covenants and warns that serving their gods becomes a stumbling block.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 23:32 You shall make no covenant with them, nor with their gods;

    Exodus 23:33 neither shall they dwell in your land, lest they cause you to sin against Me; for if you serve their gods, these shall be a stumbling block to you.”

    Pinned source record

  2. Deuteronomy 7:2-5 No covenant and destruction of cult objects commanded

    Both forbid a covenant with the land's peoples and command destruction of their altars and images; Deuteronomy additionally addresses intermarriage, pillars and groves rather than being treated as an identical quotation.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 7:2 and the Lord your God delivers them into your hands, and you strike them, you shall utterly destroy them. You shall make no covenant with them, neither shall you show them mercy.

    Deuteronomy 7:3 Neither shall you intermarry with them; you shall not give your daughter unto his son, nor take his daughter for your son.

    Deuteronomy 7:4 For he will turn your son away from Me, and he will serve other gods; and the Lord shall be kindled to wrath against you, and shall destroy you speedily.

    Deuteronomy 7:5 But you shall do thus unto them: you shall pull down their altars, and break their pillars, and cut down their sacred groves, and burn the graven images of their gods with fire.

    Pinned source record

Judges 2:3

And I said, ‘I will no longer remove the people whom I said I would destroy before you. They shall become constraints upon you, and their gods shall become a stumbling block to you.’”

  1. Joshua 23:12-13 Retained nations and their gods as a threatened snare

    Both warn that the Lord will cease removing remaining nations; Joshua calls those nations traps and stumbling blocks, while Judges calls the peoples constraints and their gods a stumbling block, preserving the distinct subjects and images.

    Read matching passage

    Joshua 23:12 For if you turn back and cling to these nations that remain with you, and intermarry with them, and mingle with them and they with you,

    Joshua 23:13 know that the Lord shall surely no longer destroy these nations before you. They shall be traps and stumbling blocks to you, and nails in your heels and darts in your eyes, until you perish from this good land which the Lord your God has given you.

    Pinned source record
